\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{n}^{\sigma},\mu^{\sigma})+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}|\theta_{n}^{\top}X-\theta^{\top}X|^{2}},

where
\mu_{n,\theta_{n}}^{\sigma}:=(\mu_{n})_{\theta_{n}}^{\sigma}
and
\mu_{n}^{\sigma}:=(\mu_{n})^{\sigma}
. Taking
n\to\infty
and using the dominated convergence theorem, we conclude our desired continuity. It also implies the continuity of the map
\mathcal{V}\times\mathcal{S}^{d-1}\ni(\nu^{\sigma},\theta)\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}}|y|^{2}\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}(dy)
and hence
C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}:=\displaystyle\sup_{\nu^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{V},\theta\in\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|y|^{2}\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}(dy)<\infty
by the compactness of
\mathcal{V}
. By Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle F_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(z)\leq C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}/z^{2},\text{ for any $z<0$},

and hence
for
z=F^{-1}_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(y)<0
with
0<y<F_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(0)
, we have

|F^{-1}_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(y)|\leq\sqrt{C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}/y},\text{ for $0<y<F_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(0)$}.

(4.2)

Let
m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}
be the median of
\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}
, i.e.,
m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}:=F^{-1}_{\mu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(1/2)
. We have that
C_{\mathcal{U},\sigma}:=\hskip-3.0pt\displaystyle\sup_{\mu^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{U},\theta\in\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\hskip-3.0pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}F^{-1}_{\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(1/2)\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}<\infty
due to the continuity of the map
\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{S}^{d-1}\ni(\mu^{\sigma},\theta)\mapsto F^{-1}_{\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(1/2)
(see
[
41
, Lemma 21.2]
). Therefore, there is a large enough positive constant
L_{\mathcal{U},\sigma}>0
depending only on
\sigma
,
\mathcal{U}
such that for any
z<-L_{\mathcal{U},\sigma}
,
\mu^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{U}
and
\theta\in\mathcal{S}^{d-1}
, we have

\displaystyle F_{\mu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(z)

\displaystyle=\int^{\infty}_{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}F_{\mu_{\theta}}(z-y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy+\int_{-\infty}^{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}F_{\mu_{\theta}}(z-y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy

\displaystyle\geq\int_{-\infty}^{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}F_{\mu_{\theta}}(m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu})\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{\sigma^{2}}{2|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|}\left(1-\dfrac{\sigma^{2}}{|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{2}}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{\sigma^{2}}{4|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|}\exp\left(-\frac{|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right),

(4.3)

where the second last inequality comes from, for instance,
[
25
, Theorem 1.2.6]
. Note that
\displaystyle\inf_{\nu^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{V},\theta\in\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\hskip-3.0ptF_{\nu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(0)>0
since every measures
\nu^{\sigma}
is Gaussian regularized, and the infimum can actually be attained due to compactness. There is a
L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}\geq L_{\mathcal{U},\sigma}
such that
0<F_{\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(z)<F_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(0)
for any
z<-L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}
. Thus, by (
4.2
),

\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}F^{-1}_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(F_{\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(z))\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}\leq\dfrac{2C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}^{1/2}|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{1/2}}{\sigma}\exp\left(\frac{|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\right)

for any
z<-L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}
. The case for the right tail is similar. By Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle|F_{\nu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}^{-1}(y)|\leq\sqrt{C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}/(1-y)},\text{ for }F_{\nu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(0)<y<1.

(4.4)

Taking
L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}>0
large enough, we see that for any
z>L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}
,

\displaystyle F_{\mu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(z)

\displaystyle=\int^{\infty}_{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}F_{\mu_{\theta}}(z-y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy+\int_{-\infty}^{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}F_{\mu_{\theta}}(z-y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy+\int_{-\infty}^{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy

\displaystyle=1-\int_{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy,

and therefore

\displaystyle 1-F_{\mu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(z)\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(y)dy.

By the symmetry of the distribution of
\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}
, a similar estimate as (
4.1
) can be derived. Therefore, together with (
4.2
) and (
4.4
), we have that for any
|z|>L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}
,

\displaystyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\big|$}}}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}=\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}F^{-1}_{\nu^{\sigma}_{\theta}}(F_{\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(z))\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}\leq\dfrac{2C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}^{1/2}|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{1/2}}{\sigma}\exp\left(\frac{|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\right).

Since both the distribution and quantile functions of probability measures in
\mathcal{U}
,
\mathcal{V}
are continuous and strictly increasing on the spatial variable,
T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)
is strictly increasing. For the region
|z|\leq L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}
, as the map
T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)
is continuous on
\mu
and
\theta
,
K:=\displaystyle\sup_{\mu^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{U},\theta\in\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}|T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma})|\vee|T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(-L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma})|<\infty
. Since
T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)
is strictly increasing in
z
, we have

C_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}:=\displaystyle\sup_{\mu^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{U},\theta\in\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\displaystyle\sup_{|z|\leq L_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}}|T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)|<\infty
. Therefore, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt|T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)|\exp\left(-\dfrac{|z-\theta^{\top}x|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[C_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}\exp\left(-\dfrac{|z-\theta^{\top}x|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)\right]\vee\left[\dfrac{2C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}^{1/2}|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{1/2}}{\sigma}\exp\left(\frac{|z-m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|^{2}-2|z-\theta^{\top}x|^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\right)\right].

Recall that
|m_{\sigma,\theta,\mu}|=|F_{\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}^{-1}(1/2)|\leq C_{\mathcal{U},\sigma}
. Thus, we can use Young’s inequality to obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt|T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)|\exp\left(-\dfrac{|z-\theta^{\top}x|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[C_{\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\sigma}\exp\left(\dfrac{-z^{2}/2+|\theta^{\top}x|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)\right]\vee\left[\dfrac{2C_{\mathcal{V},\sigma}^{1/2}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}|z|^{1/2}+C_{\mathcal{U},\sigma}^{1/2}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}}{\sigma}\exp\left(\frac{-z^{2}/2+14|\theta^{\top}x|^{2}+5C_{\mathcal{U},\sigma}^{2}}{4\sigma^{2}}\right)\right].

As
x
is in the compact set
\mathcal{X}
, we conclude the required result.
∎

We have the following lemmas for the derivatives of
\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}
, when fixing
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\sigma>0
:

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\sigma>0
be fixed. It holds that

1.

the mapping
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\ni\mu\mapsto\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}

is L-differentiable with

\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\Big\{\theta^{\top}x-\mathbb{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}(x+N_{\sigma}))\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}\Big\}d\theta;

2.

there is a constant
C_{d}>0
depending only on
d
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)\right|^{2}\mu(dx)\leq C_{d}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu(dx)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|y|^{2}\nu^{\sigma}(dy)\Big);

3.

the mapping
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\ni(x,\mu)\mapsto\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)
is jointly continuous.

Proof.

We follow
[
4
, Lemma 2.2]
to obtain items (1) and (2). To prove (3), we let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\{\mu_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
|x_{n}-x|\to 0
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{n},\mu)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. It is sufficient to consider

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\Big\{\mathbb{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}T_{\theta,\mu_{n}}^{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}(x_{n}+N_{\sigma})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}-T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}(x+N_{\sigma})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}\Big\}d\theta

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\Big\{\mathbb{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}T_{\theta,\mu_{n}}^{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}x_{n}+N_{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}-T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}x+N_{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}\Big\}d\theta

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[T_{\theta,\mu_{n}}^{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}x_{n}+y\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}-T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}x+y\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}\Big]\dfrac{1}{(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{1/2}}\exp\left(-\dfrac{y^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)dyd\theta

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{1/2}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\theta T_{\theta,\mu_{n}}^{\sigma}(y)\exp\left(-\dfrac{|y-\theta^{\top}x_{n}|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)-\theta T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\exp\left(-\dfrac{|y-\theta^{\top}x|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)dyd\theta,

where the normal random variable in the first line is
d
-dimensional and that in the second line is
1
-dimensional. By Lemma
4.4
, the pointwise convergence of
T^{\sigma}_{\theta,\mu_{n}}(z)
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we conclude that the above term converges to zero as
n\to\infty
, which implies the desired continuity.

∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\sigma>0
be fixed. It holds that

1.

for each
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the mapping
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)

is differentiable with respect to
x
with

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}\left(1-\dfrac{d}{dz}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(z)\Big|_{z=\theta^{\top}\left(x+N_{\sigma}\right)}\right)d\theta\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}d\theta+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\left(\theta\theta^{\top}\frac{(\theta^{\top}x-y)}{\sigma^{2}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x-y)\right)d\theta dy;

2.

there is a constant
C_{d}>0
depending only on
d
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)\right|^{2}\mu(dx)\leq C_{d}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\nu^{\sigma}(dx)\right);

3.

the mapping
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\ni(x,\mu)\mapsto\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)
is jointly continuous.

Proof.

Item (1) is obtained by directly differentiating (1) in Lemma
4.5
and the integration by part. The interchange of the differentiation and the integration is justified in the proof of
[
4
, Lemma 2.4]
. To prove (2), we use (
4.1
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}(x)\right|^{2}\mu(dx)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}d\theta+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\left(\theta\theta^{\top}\frac{(\theta^{\top}x-y)}{\sigma^{2}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x-y)\right)d\theta dy\right|^{2}\mu(dx)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle 2\left|\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}d\theta\right|^{2}+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\left(\theta\theta^{\top}\frac{(\theta^{\top}x-y)}{\sigma^{2}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x-y)\right)d\theta dy\right|^{2}\mu(dx)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle C_{d}+\frac{2}{\sigma^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\!\!(\theta^{\top}x-y)^{2}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x-y)d\theta dy\right)\!\!\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\!\!\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x-y)d\theta dy\right)\mu(dx)

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle C_{d}\left(1+\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\nu^{\sigma}(dx)\right).

To prove (3), we let
\{x_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\{\mu_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
|x_{n}-x|\to 0
and
\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{n},\mu)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. It is sufficient to consider

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}\left(\frac{(\theta^{\top}x_{n}-y)}{\sigma^{2}}T_{\theta,\mu_{n}}^{\sigma}(y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x_{n}-y)-\frac{(\theta^{\top}x-y)}{\sigma^{2}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x-y)\right)d\theta dy

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}\frac{(\theta^{\top}x_{n}-\theta^{\top}x)}{\sigma^{2}}T_{\theta,\mu_{n}}^{\sigma}(y)\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x_{n}-y)d\theta dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}\frac{(\theta^{\top}x-y)}{\sigma^{2}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}T_{\theta,\mu_{n}}^{\sigma}(y)-T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x_{n}-y)d\theta dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}\frac{(\theta^{\top}x-y)}{\sigma^{2}}T_{\theta,\mu}^{\sigma}(y)\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x_{n}-y)-\mathcal{N}_{\sigma}(\theta^{\top}x-y)\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}d\theta dy.

By Lemma
4.4
, the pointwise convergence of
T^{\sigma}_{\theta,\mu_{n}}(y)
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we conclude that the above term converges to zero as
n\to\infty
, which implies the desired continuity.
∎

We have the following estimate when acting
\mathcal{H}
on the metric:

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\sigma>0
be fixed. We have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{H}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}\Big|\leq C_{d}.

Proof.

For ease of notation, let
h
denote the map:

\displaystyle h:\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}\,;\,\mu\longmapsto h(\mu):=\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}.

For each
X\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P};\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\mu=\mathcal{L}(X)
, we denote
T_{\theta,X}^{\sigma}
to be the map
T_{\theta,X}^{\sigma}(x):=F_{\nu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}^{-1}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}F_{\mu_{\theta}^{\sigma}}(x)\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}
. For
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\mu=\mathcal{L}(X)
, we also denote
h^{*}
to be the lifting of
h
to Hilbert space, then
h^{*}(X+w)=h((I_{d}+w)_{\sharp}\mu)
, where
I_{d}+w:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the map
x\mapsto x+w
for
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dw}h^{*}(X+w)=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\mu}h(\mathcal{L}(X+w))(X+w)],

where
\dfrac{d}{dw}
is the usual gradient operator on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Therefore, as the laws of
T^{\sigma}_{\theta,X+w}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}(X+w+\bar{N}_{\sigma})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}
and
T^{\sigma}_{\theta,X}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}(X+\bar{N}_{\sigma})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}
are the same by (
4.1
), we have

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\mu}h(\mathcal{L}(X+w))(X+w)]-\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\mu}h(\mathcal{L}(X))(X)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\left\{\theta^{\top}(X+w)-\bar{\mathbb{E}}\Big[T^{\sigma}_{\theta,X+w}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}(X+w+\bar{N}_{\sigma})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}\Big]\right\}d\theta

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\left\{\theta^{\top}X-\bar{\mathbb{E}}\Big[T^{\sigma}_{\theta,X}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\theta^{\top}(X+\bar{N}_{\sigma})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}\Big]\right\}d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}wd\theta,

and thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}h=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}d\theta,

from which
|\mathcal{H}h|\leq C_{d}
is obvious.
∎

For each
\sigma>0
, we define
\rho_{\sigma}:([0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle\rho_{\sigma}((s,\mu),(t,\nu)):=|t-s|^{2}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}^{\sigma}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}.

(4.5)

It is a gauge type function on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}([0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),(\rho_{\sigma})^{1/2})}
by Definition
4.1
. We conclude the following smooth variational principle:

Theorem 4.8
.

Fix
\delta>0
and let
G:[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be upper semicontinuous and bounded from above. If there exists
\lambda>0
and
\left(t_{0},\mu_{0}\right)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\sup_{(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}G(t,\mu)-\lambda\leq G\left(t_{0},\mu_{0}\right),

then there exists
(\tilde{t},\tilde{\mu})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and a sequence
\left\{\left(t_{n},\mu_{n}\right)\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that:

1.

\rho_{1/\delta}\left((\tilde{t},\tilde{\mu}),\left(t_{n},\mu_{n}\right)\right)\leq\dfrac{\lambda}{2^{n}\delta^{2}}
for any
n=0,1,2,\ldots
;

2.

G\left(t_{0},\mu_{0}\right)\leq G(\tilde{t},\tilde{\mu})-\delta^{2}\varphi_{\delta}(\tilde{t},\tilde{\mu})
with
\varphi_{\delta}:[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
given by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\delta}(t,\mu):=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{2^{n}}\rho_{1/\delta}\left((t,\mu),\left(t_{n},\mu_{n}\right)\right),\quad\text{for any }(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d});

(4.6)

3.

G(t,\mu)-\delta^{2}\varphi_{\delta}(t,\mu)<G(\tilde{t},\tilde{\mu})-\delta^{2}\varphi_{\delta}(\tilde{t},\tilde{\mu})
, for every
(t,\mu)\in\left([0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\right)\backslash\{(\tilde{t},\tilde{\mu})\}
;

4.

\varphi_{\delta}\in PC_{1}^{1,2}([0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and there exists a constant
C_{d}>0
depending only on
d
such that for any
(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\varphi_{\delta}(t,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq 4T;

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\partial_{\mu}\varphi_{\delta}(t,\mu)(x)\right|^{2}\mu(dx)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\left(\frac{1+\lambda}{\delta^{2}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu(dx)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu_{0}(dx)\right);

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\varphi_{\delta}(t,\mu)(x)\right|^{2}\mu(dx)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\delta^{2}\Big(\frac{1+\lambda}{\delta^{2}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu_{0}(dx)\Big);

\displaystyle|\mathcal{H}\varphi_{\delta}(t,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}.

Proof.

For items (1) to (3), we refer readers to
[
29
, Theorem 2.5.2]
. As there is a constant
\kappa_{d}>0
depending only on
d
such that
\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\theta\theta^{\top}d\theta=\kappa_{d}I_{d}
, by a simple application of the triangle inequality we conclude that

\displaystyle\kappa_{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\nu(dx)=\int_{\mathcal{S}^{d-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|y|^{2}\nu_{\theta}(dy)d\theta\leq C_{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu(dx)+\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}(\mu,\nu)\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}\right).

(4.7)

The constant
C_{d}
changes from line to line in this proof, but it still depends only on
d
. Replacing
\nu
and
\mu
with
\mu_{n}^{1/\delta}
and
\mu^{1/\delta}_{0}
respectively in the above, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu_{n}^{1/\delta}(dx)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu^{1/\delta}_{0}(dx)+\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}(\mu^{1/\delta}_{n},\tilde{\mu}^{1/\delta})\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}+\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}SW_{2}(\tilde{\mu}^{1/\delta},\mu^{1/\delta}_{0})\mathclose{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu_{0}(dx)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|y|^{2}\mathcal{N}_{1/\delta}(dy)+\frac{\lambda}{2^{n}\delta^{2}}+\frac{\lambda}{\delta^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu_{0}(dx)+\frac{1+\lambda}{\delta^{2}}+\frac{\lambda}{2^{n}\delta^{2}}\right).

(4.8)

From Lemmas
4.5
-
4.7
, we see that the derivatives
\partial_{t}\rho_{1/\delta}\left((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n})\right)
,
\partial_{\mu}\rho_{1/\delta}\left((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n})\right)(x)
,
\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\rho_{1/\delta}\left((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n})\right)(x)
and
\mathcal{H}\rho_{1/\delta}\left((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n})\right)
exist for each
n=0,1,2,\ldots
and also

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}\rho_{1/\delta}((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n}))|=2t\hskip 5.0pt\text{and}\hskip 5.0pt\left|\mathcal{H}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big($}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big($}}}}}\rho_{1/\delta}((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n}))\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big)$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{327680}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big)$}}}}}\right|\leq C_{d}.

(4.9)

The interchange of the differentiation and the infinite sum for
\partial_{t}\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)
and
\mathcal{H}\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)
are obvious by the above bounds and Lemma
4.4
. Therefore,
\partial_{t}\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)
and
\mathcal{H}\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)
exist and have the desired upper bounds by using the definition of
\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)
and (
4.9
). Moreover, we are safe to interchange the measure differentiation and the infinite sum for
\partial_{\mu}\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)(x)
by the proof of
[
4
, Proposition 2.5]
, thus the derivative
\partial_{\mu}\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)(x)
also exists. According to Lemmas
4.4
and
4.6
, we see that the derivative
\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)(x)
also exists by interchanging the differentiation and the infinite sum. The joint continuity of these derivatives follows from the dominating function constructed in Lemma
4.4
, (3) in Lemma
4.5
, (3) in Lemma
4.6
, Lemma
4.7
, the definition of
\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)
and the dominated convergence theorem. Finally, from Lemmas
4.5
-
4.7
and (
4.8
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{\mu}\rho_{1/\delta}((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n}))(x)|^{2}\mu(dx)\leq\,

\displaystyle C_{d}\Big(\frac{1+\lambda}{\delta^{2}}+\frac{\lambda}{2^{n}\delta^{2}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu(dx)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu_{0}(dx)\Big),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\rho_{1/\delta}((t,\mu),(t_{n},\mu_{n}))(x)|^{2}\mu(dx)\leq\,

\displaystyle C_{d}\Big(1+\lambda+\dfrac{\lambda}{2^{n}}+\delta^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\mu_{0}(dx)\Big).

By the above bounds, (
4.9
) and the definition of
\varphi_{1/\delta}(t,\mu)
, we conclude the desired bounds.
∎

5
Smooth Finite-dimensional Approximations of the Value
Function

This section is to carry out the smooth finite-dimensional approximations of the value function through the
n
-particles approximation of the measure argument and the mollification of the coefficient functions
b
,
f
and
g
.

5.1
Infinite-dimensional Approximation

Fix a complete probability space
(\check{\Omega},\check{\mathcal{F}},\check{\mathbb{P}})
, also of the form
(\check{\Omega}^{0}\times\check{\Omega}^{1},\check{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\otimes\check{\mathcal{F}}^{1},\check{\mathbb{P}}^{0}\otimes\check{\mathbb{P}}^{1})
. The space
(\check{\Omega}^{0},\check{\mathcal{F}}^{0},\check{\mathbb{P}}^{0})
supports a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion
\check{W}^{0}
. For
(\check{\Omega}^{1},\check{\mathcal{F}}^{1},\check{\mathbb{P}}^{1})
, it is of the form
(\check{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1}\times\check{\hat{\Omega}}^{1},\check{\mathcal{G}}\otimes\check{\hat{\mathcal{F}}}^{1},\check{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}^{1}\otimes\check{\hat{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
. On
(\check{\hat{\Omega}}^{1},\check{\hat{\mathcal{F}}}^{1},\check{\hat{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
, there lives
d
-dimensional Brownian motions
\check{W}
and
\check{B}
. The space
(\check{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1},\check{\mathcal{G}},\check{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
is where the initial random variables live. We assume that
(\check{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1},\check{\mathcal{G}},\check{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
is rich enough to support all probability laws in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e., for any probability law
\mu
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, there exists
X\in\check{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1}
such that
\mathcal{L}(X)=\mu
. The expectation
\mathbb{E}
is taken with respect to
\check{\mathbb{P}}^{0}\otimes\check{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
.

Set
\check{\mathbb{F}}=(\check{\mathcal{F}}_{s})_{s\geq 0}:=\left(\sigma(\check{W}^{0}_{r})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\sigma(\check{W}_{r})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\sigma(\check{B}_{r})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\check{\mathcal{G}}\right)_{s\geq 0}
,
\check{\mathbb{F}}^{t}=(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{t})_{s\geq 0}:=\big(\sigma(\check{W}_{r}^{0})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\sigma(\check{W}_{r\vee t}-\check{W}_{t})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\sigma(\check{B}_{r\vee t}-\check{B}_{t})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\check{\mathcal{G}}\big)_{s\geq 0}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that they are
\check{\mathbb{P}}
-complete. Let
t>0
, denote
\check{\mathcal{A}}
(resp.
\check{\mathcal{A}}_{t}
) the set of
\check{\mathbb{F}}
-progressively measurable process (resp.
\check{\mathbb{F}}^{t}
-progressively measurable process)
\check{\alpha}
valued in
A
.

Letting
\varepsilon>0
,
t\in[0,T)
,
\check{\xi}\in L^{2}(\check{\Omega}^{1},\check{\mathcal{F}}^{1},\check{\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\check{\alpha}\in\check{\mathcal{A}}
, we consider the unique solution
\check{X}^{\varepsilon,t,\check{\xi},\check{\alpha}}=(\check{X}^{\varepsilon,t,\check{\xi},\check{\alpha}}_{s})_{s\in[t,T]}
of the system of the perturbed equation:

\displaystyle X_{s}=\,

\displaystyle\check{\xi}+\int_{t}^{s}b(r,X_{r},\mathbb{P}_{X_{r}}^{\check{W}^{0}},\check{\alpha}_{r})dr+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma(r,X_{r},\check{\alpha}_{r})d\check{W}_{r}+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma^{0}(r)d\check{W}^{0}_{r}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon(\check{B}_{s}-\check{B}_{t}).

(5.1)

For any
t\in[0,T]
and
\check{\xi}\in L^{2}(\check{\Omega}^{1},\check{\mathcal{F}}^{1},\check{\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we consider the value function:

\displaystyle V_{\varepsilon}(t,\check{\xi})=\sup_{\check{\alpha}\in\check{\mathcal{A}}_{t}}J_{\varepsilon}(t,\check{\xi},\check{\alpha}):=\sup_{\check{\alpha}\in\check{\mathcal{A}}_{t}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{t}^{T}f\Big(s,\check{X}^{\varepsilon,t,\check{\xi},\check{\alpha}}_{s},\mathbb{P}^{\check{W}^{0}}_{\check{X}^{\varepsilon,t,\check{\xi},\check{\alpha}}_{s}},\check{\alpha}_{s}\Big)ds+g\Big(\check{X}^{\varepsilon,t,\check{\xi},\check{\alpha}}_{T},\mathbb{P}^{\check{W}^{0}}_{\check{X}^{\varepsilon,t,\check{\xi},\check{\alpha}}_{T}}\Big)\Bigg].

(5.2)

By the law invariance property, we can define a function
v_{\varepsilon}(t,\mu):[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle v_{\varepsilon}(t,\mu):=V_{\varepsilon}(t,\check{\xi}),

(5.3)

for any
\check{\xi}\in L^{2}(\check{\Omega}^{1},\check{\mathcal{F}}^{1},\check{\mathbb{P}}^{1};\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\mathcal{L}(\check{\xi})=\mu
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Suppose that Assumption (A) holds. There exists a constant
C_{5}=C_{5}(d,K,T)
such that for any
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, it holds that
|v_{\varepsilon}(t,\mu)-v_{0}(t,\mu)|\leq C_{5}\varepsilon.

The proof is standard by using Assumption (A), equation (
5.1
) and the definition in (
5.2
). We omit it here.

5.2
Finite-dimensional Approximation

In this section, we will illustrate the finite-dimensional approximation. Consider a complete probability space
(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathcal{F}},\overline{\mathbb{P}})
, it is also of the form
(\overline{\Omega}^{0}\times\overline{\Omega}^{1},\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\otimes\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{1},\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{0}\otimes\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{1})
. The space
(\overline{\Omega}^{0},\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{0},\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{0})
supports a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion
\overline{W}^{0}
. For
(\overline{\Omega}^{1},\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{1},\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{1})
, it is of the form
(\overline{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1}\times\overline{\hat{\Omega}}^{1},\overline{\mathcal{G}}\otimes\overline{\hat{\mathcal{F}}}^{1},\overline{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}^{1}\otimes\overline{\hat{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there lives
d
-dimensional Brownian motions
\overline{W}^{1},\ldots,\overline{W}^{n}
and
\overline{B}^{1},\ldots,\overline{B}^{n}
on
(\overline{\hat{\Omega}}^{1},\overline{\hat{\mathcal{F}}}^{1},\overline{\hat{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
. We require
\overline{W}^{1},\ldots,\overline{W}^{n},\overline{B}^{1},\ldots,\overline{B}^{n}
to be mutually independent. The space
(\overline{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1},\overline{\mathcal{G}},\overline{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
is where the initial random variables live. We assume that
(\overline{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1},\overline{\mathcal{G}},\overline{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}^{1})
is rich enough to support all probability laws in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, i.e., for any probability law
\mu
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, there exists
X\in\overline{\tilde{\Omega}}^{1}
such that
\mathcal{L}(X)=\mu
.

Put
\overline{\mathbb{F}}=(\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{s})_{s\geq 0}:=\left(\sigma(\overline{W}^{0}_{r})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\sigma(\overline{W}_{r}^{i})_{0\leq r\leq s,i=1,\ldots,n}\vee\sigma(\overline{B}_{r}^{i})_{0\leq r\leq s,i=1,\ldots,n}\vee\overline{\mathcal{G}}\right)_{s\geq 0}
,
\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{t}=(\mathcal{F}_{s}^{t})_{s\geq 0}:=\left(\sigma(\overline{W}_{r}^{0})_{0\leq r\leq s}\vee\sigma(\overline{W}_{r\vee t}^{i}-\overline{W}^{i}_{t})_{0\leq r\leq s,i=1,\ldots,n}\vee\sigma(\overline{B}^{i}_{r\vee t}-\overline{B}^{i}_{t})_{0\leq r\leq s,i=1,\ldots,n}\vee\overline{\mathcal{G}}\right)_{s\geq 0}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that they are
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
-complete. Let
t>0
and denote
\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}
(resp.
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{n}
) the set of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}
-progressively measurable process (resp.
\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{t}
-progressively measurable process)
\overline{\alpha}=(\overline{\alpha}^{1},\ldots,\overline{\alpha}^{n})
valued in
A^{n}
.

Let
\overline{\xi}=(\overline{\xi}^{1},\ldots,\overline{\xi}^{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{dn}
, we consider the solution
\overline{X}^{\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s}=(\overline{X}^{1,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s},\ldots,\overline{X}^{n,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{dn}
of the system of the perturbed equation:

\displaystyle X_{s}^{i}=\,

\displaystyle\overline{\xi}^{i}+\int_{t}^{s}b(r,X_{r}^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n}_{r},\overline{\alpha}_{r}^{i})dr+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma(r,X_{r}^{i},\overline{\alpha}_{r}^{i})d\overline{W}_{r}^{i}+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma^{0}(r)d\overline{W}^{0}_{r}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon(\overline{B}^{i}_{s}-\overline{B}^{i}_{t}),

(5.4)

for each
i=1,2,\ldots,n
and
X_{s}=(X^{1}_{s},\ldots,X^{n}_{s})
with

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}^{n}_{r}=\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\delta_{\overline{X}^{i}_{r}}.

Denoting
\widehat{\mu}^{n,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s}:=\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum^{n}_{i=1}\delta_{\overline{X}^{i,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s}}
, we consider the value function:

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon,n}(t,\overline{\mu})

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}_{t}}J_{\varepsilon,n}(t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha})

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}_{t}}\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{t}^{T}f(s,\overline{X}^{i,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s},\widehat{\mu}^{\,n,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s},\overline{\alpha}^{i}_{s})ds+g(\overline{X}^{i,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{T},\widehat{\mu}^{\,n,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{T})\Bigg],

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\overline{\mu}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{dn})
such that
\mathcal{L}({\overline{\xi}})=\overline{\mu}
.

Now we introduce the smooth approximation of the coefficients. For
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
i=1,\ldots,n
, we define
b^{i}_{n,m}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{dn}\times A\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

f^{i}_{n,m}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{dn}\times A\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

g^{i}_{n,m}:\mathbb{R}^{dn}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the smooth approximation of
b
,
f
and
g
respectively such that

\displaystyle b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)

\displaystyle:=m^{dn+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn+1}}b\left((t-s)^{+}\wedge T,x^{i}-y^{i},\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}},a\right)\phi(ms)\prod^{n}_{k=1}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}ds;

(5.5)

\displaystyle f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)

\displaystyle:=m^{dn+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn+1}}f\left((t-s)^{+}\wedge T,x^{i}-y^{i},\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}},a\right)\phi(ms)\prod^{n}_{k=1}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}ds;

(5.6)

\displaystyle g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})

\displaystyle:=m^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}g\left(x^{i}-y^{i},\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\right)\prod^{n}_{k=1}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k},

(5.7)

where
t\in[0,T]
,
a\in A
and
\overline{x}=(x^{1},\ldots,x^{n})
. The functions
\phi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
,
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
are two compactly supported smooth functions satisfying
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(s)ds=1
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Phi(y)dy=1
. First, we establish some basic properties of these approximation functions.

Lemma 5.2
.

Suppose that
Assumption (A*)
holds and define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}}:=\dfrac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum^{n}_{j=1}\delta_{x^{j}}.

For any
i=1,2,\ldots,n
, we have the following

1.

Upper Bound:

for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
(t,\overline{x},a)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{dn}\times A
, we have

\displaystyle|b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)|\vee|f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)|\vee|g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})|\leq K;

2.

Convergence:
it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)=b(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a),\hskip 10.0pt\lim_{m\to\infty}f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)=f(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})=g(x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}}),

uniformly in
(t,\overline{x},a)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{dn}\times A
;

3.

Convergence Rate:
for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
(t,\overline{x},a)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{dn}\times A
, we have the estimates

(a)

for
b
and
f
:

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt|b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)-b(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a)|\vee|f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)-f(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a)|

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle Km\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|t-\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big[$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big[$}}}}}T\wedge(t-s)^{+}\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big]$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{285200}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big]$}}}}}\right|^{\beta}\phi(ms)ds+Km^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}\left(|y^{i}|+\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{j=1}|y^{j}|\right)\prod^{n}_{k=1}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k};

(b)

for
g
:

\displaystyle|g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})-g(x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}})|

\displaystyle\leq Km^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}\left(|y^{i}|+\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{j=1}|y^{j}|\right)\prod^{n}_{k=1}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k};

4.

Continuity:
for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
(t,\overline{x},\overline{x}^{\prime},a)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{dn}\times\mathbb{R}^{dn}\times A
, we have the estimate

\displaystyle|b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)-b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x}^{\prime},a)|\vee|f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)-f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x}^{\prime},a)|\vee|g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})-g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq K\left[|x^{i}-x^{\prime i}|+\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\left|x^{j}-x^{\prime j}\right|\right].

(5.8)

Proof.

Assertion (1) is obvious by the definitions in (
5.5
)-(
5.7
) and Assumption (A*). We prove assertion (3) for
g
by considering

\displaystyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\big|$}}}}g(x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}})-g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}\leq m^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}\bigg|g(x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}})-g\bigg(x^{i}-y^{i},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)\bigg|\prod^{n}_{k=1}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}.

Using the Lipschitz continuity of
g
in Assumption (A*) and the fact that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{1}\bigg(\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{W}_{1}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x-y|\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{(x^{j},x^{j}-y^{j})}(dx,dy)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|y^{j}|,

(5.9)

we obtain that

\displaystyle\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\big|$}}}}g(x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}})-g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})\mathchoice{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\displaystyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\textstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\big|$}}}}}{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{\resizebox{291272}{786432}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\big|$}}}}}\leq Km^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}\bigg(|y^{i}|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|y^{j}|\bigg)\prod_{k=1}^{n}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}.

We prove assertion (3) for
b
by considering (the proof for
f
is exactly the same)

\displaystyle|b(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a)-b_{n,m}^{i}(t,\overline{x},a)|

\displaystyle\leq m^{dn+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn+1}}\left|b(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a)-b\bigg(T\wedge(t-s)^{+},x^{i}-y^{i},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}},a\bigg)\right|\phi(ms)\prod_{k=1}^{n}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}ds

\displaystyle\leq m\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|b(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a)-b\left(T\wedge(t-s)^{+},x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a\right)\right|\phi(ms)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+m^{dn+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn+1}}\left|b\left(T\wedge(t-s)^{+},x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a\right)-b\bigg(T\wedge(t-s)^{+},x^{i}-y^{i},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}},a\bigg)\right|\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 335.0pt\phi(ms)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\Phi(my^{j})dy^{j}ds.

The inequality in (
5.9
) and Assumption (A*) implies that

\displaystyle|b(t,x^{i},\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},a)-b_{n,m}^{i}(t,\overline{x},a)|

\displaystyle\leq Km\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|t-(T\wedge(t-s)^{+}))\right|^{\beta}\phi(ms)ds+Km^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}\bigg(|y^{i}|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|y^{j}|\bigg)\prod_{k=1}^{n}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}.

Assertion (2) follows immediately from assertion (3). For the proof of assertion (4) for
g
(the proofs for
f
and
b
are exactly the same), we let
\overline{x}
,
\overline{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{dn}
and estimate

\displaystyle|g_{n,m}^{i}(\overline{x})-g_{n,m}^{i}(\overline{z})|

\displaystyle\leq m^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}\bigg|g\bigg(x^{i}-y^{i},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)-g\bigg(z^{i}-y^{i},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{z^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)\bigg|\prod_{k=1}^{n}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}.

Then the inequality in (
5.9
) yields that

\displaystyle|g_{n,m}^{i}(\overline{x})-g_{n,m}^{i}(\overline{z})|

\displaystyle\leq Km^{dn}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{dn}}\bigg[|x^{i}-z^{i}|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|x^{j}-z^{j}|\bigg]\prod_{k=1}^{n}\Phi(my^{k})dy^{k}

\displaystyle=K\bigg[|x^{i}-z^{i}|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|x^{j}-z^{j}|\bigg].

∎

Remark 5.1
.

It is worth noting that the coefficients
b
,
f
and
g
are assumed to be
\mathcal{W}_{1}
-Lipschitz continuous in Assumption (A*), which is stronger than the
\mathcal{W}_{2}
-Lipschitz continuity assumed in
[
17
, Assumption (A)]
. The main reason is to provide a better Lipschitz estimate when we establish the smooth approximation of the coefficients in Lemma
5.2
, which is critical in the proof of Lemma
5.5
and Theorem
6.3
. We note that there are some gaps in the proofs of
[
17
, Theorem 5.1, Theorem A.7]
if only
\mathcal{W}_{2}
-Lipschitz continuity is assumed in
[
17
, Assumption (A)]
. More precisely, if we assume the coefficients are only
\mathcal{W}_{2}
-Lipschitz continuous, then we only have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}\bigg(\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)=\mathcal{W}_{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|y^{j}|,

(5.10)

instead of the estimate in (
5.9
). It seems that the corresponding estimate of
\mathcal{W}_{2}\Big(\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\Big)
in the proof of
[
17
, Lemma A.3]
is not true, see the counterexample at the end of this remark and the estimate in (
5.12
). Hence, adopting the
\mathcal{W}_{2}
-Lipschitz continuity and the estimate in (
5.10
), assertion (3) of Lemma
5.2
becomes

\displaystyle|b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)-b^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x}^{\prime},a)|\vee|f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x},a)-f^{i}_{n,m}(t,\overline{x}^{\prime},a)|\vee|g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x})-g^{i}_{n,m}(\overline{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq K\left[|x^{i}-x^{\prime i}|+\dfrac{1}{n^{1/2}}\sum^{n}_{j=1}\left|x^{j}-x^{\prime j}\right|\right],

(5.11)

see the corresponding estimate in
[
17
, (A.7)]
. This causes a problem when we apply Lemma
5.2
to prove Lemma
5.5
and the bound of the first order derivative in (
5.18
) (see also the corresponding estimate in
[
17
, (A.12)]
), which is critical in the proof of Theorem
6.3
. While
[
17
, Assumption (A)]
adopts the
\mathcal{W}_{2}
-Lipschitz continuity assumption, the estimate in
[
17
, (A.7)]
is invalid and we should have (
5.11
) instead. However, by using the arguments in Lemma
5.2
,
5.5
, we showed that the estimates
[
17
, (A.6), (A.7), (A.12)]
and the proofs of
[
17
, Theorem 5.1, Theorem A.7]
should be valid if
[
17
, Assumption (A)]
adopts to the
\mathcal{W}_{1}
-Lipschitz continuity.

Here is a counterexample to the estimate of
\mathcal{W}_{2}\Big(\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\Big)
in the proof of
[
17
, Lemma A.3]
) which claims that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}\bigg(\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|y^{j}|.

(5.12)

Suppose
d=1
,
\overline{x}=(x^{1},x^{2},\ldots,x^{n})^{\top}=(0,\ldots,0)^{\top}
and
\overline{y}=(y^{1},y^{2},\ldots,y^{n})^{\top}=(y^{1},0,\ldots,0)^{\top}
. Consider the probability measures
\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}}=\delta_{0}
and
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{-y^{j}}
. As
\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}}=\delta_{0}
, all mass should be transported from/into this point only (see
[
42
, (11) in Chapter 1]
), therefore, there is only one joint distribution
\pi
such that
\pi(A\times\mathbb{R})=\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}}(A)=\delta_{0}(A)
and
\pi(\mathbb{R}\times B)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{-y^{j}}(B)
for any measurable sets
A
and
B
in
\mathbb{R}
. It is given by
\pi(A\times B)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{0}(A)\delta_{-y^{j}}(B)
. Thus, for
n>1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}\bigg(\widehat{\mu}^{n,\overline{x}},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{j}-y^{j}}\bigg)=\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|y|^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{-y^{j}}(dy)\Bigg)^{1/2}=\Bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|y^{j}|^{2}\Bigg)^{1/2}=\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|y^{j}|,

where we have used
(y^{1},y^{2},\ldots,y^{n})^{\top}=(y^{1},0,\ldots,0)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
in the last equality. It contradicts (
5.12
).

Let
\overline{X}^{m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s}=(\overline{X}^{1,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s},\ldots,\overline{X}^{n,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s})\in\mathbb{R}^{dn}
be the solution of

\displaystyle X_{s}^{i}=\,

\displaystyle\overline{\xi}^{i}+\int_{t}^{s}b_{n,m}^{i}(r,X_{r},\overline{\alpha}_{r}^{i})dr+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma(r,X_{r}^{i},\overline{\alpha}_{r}^{i})d\overline{W}_{r}^{i}+\int_{t}^{s}\sigma^{0}(r)d\overline{W}^{0}_{r}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon(\overline{B}^{i}_{s}-\overline{B}^{i}_{t}),

(5.13)

for
i=1,\dots,n
, where
X_{s}^{i}
is the
i
th-component of
X_{s}
. We define

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon,n,m}(t,\overline{\mu}):=

\displaystyle\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}_{t}}J_{\varepsilon,n}^{*}(t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}_{t}}\dfrac{1}{n}\sum^{n}_{i=1}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{t}^{T}f^{i}_{n,m}\left(s,\overline{X}^{1,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s},\ldots,\overline{X}^{n,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s},\overline{\alpha}^{i}_{s}\right)ds

\displaystyle\hskip 110.0pt+g^{i}_{n,m}\left(\overline{X}^{1,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{T},\ldots,\overline{X}^{n,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{T}\right)\Bigg],

(5.14)

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\overline{\mu}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{dn})
such that
\mathcal{L}(\overline{\xi})=\overline{\mu}
.
We define
v_{\varepsilon,n,m}(t,\mu)
,
v_{\varepsilon,n}(t,\mu):[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle v_{\varepsilon,n,m}(t,\mu):=\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon,n,m}(t,\mu\otimes\ldots\otimes\mu)\hskip 10.0pt\text{and}\hskip 10.0ptv_{\varepsilon,n}(t,\mu):=\widetilde{v}_{\varepsilon,n}(t,\mu\otimes\ldots\otimes\mu).

(5.15)

Lemma 5.3
.

Suppose that
Assumption (A*)
holds. There exists a constant
C_{6}=C_{6}(K,T,d)
such that for any
(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have
|v_{\varepsilon,n,m}(t,\mu)-v_{0,n,m}(t,\mu)|\leq C_{6}\varepsilon
.

Proof.

For every
i=1,\ldots,n
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\varepsilon\geq 0
,
t\in[0,T]
,
\overline{\xi}\in L^{2}(\overline{\Omega},\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t},\overline{\mathbb{P}};\mathbb{R}^{nd})
,
\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t}^{n}
, as
\varepsilon
is the only parameter that is varying, we write
\overline{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}
to stand for
(\overline{X}_{s}^{1;\varepsilon},\ldots,\overline{X}_{s}^{n;\varepsilon})=(\overline{X}^{1,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s},\ldots,\overline{X}^{n,m,\varepsilon,t,\overline{\xi},\overline{\alpha}}_{s})
which solves (
5.13
). Then with (
5.8
), it is easily seen that there is some constant
C_{d,K,T}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|X^{i;\varepsilon}_{s}-X^{i;0}_{s}|^{2}\right]\leq C_{d,K,T}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t}^{s}|X^{i;\varepsilon}_{r}-X^{i;0}_{r}|^{2}dr+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{t}^{s}|X^{j;\varepsilon}_{r}-X^{j;0}_{r}|^{2}dr\bigg]+\varepsilon^{2}d(s-t).

Summing on
i=1,\ldots,n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}|X^{i;\varepsilon}_{s}-X^{i;0}_{s}|^{2}\right]\leq C_{d,K,T}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{t}^{s}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|X^{i;\varepsilon}_{r}-X^{i;0}_{r}|^{2}dr\right)+n\varepsilon^{2}(s-t)\Bigg],

and hence Grönwall’s inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}|X^{i;\varepsilon}_{s}-X^{i;0}_{s}|^{2}\right]\leq C_{d,K,T}n\varepsilon^{2}(s-t)e^{C_{d,K,T}(s-t)}.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and take
\overline{\xi}=(\xi,\ldots,\xi)\in\mathbb{R}^{dn}
such that
\mathcal{L}(\xi)=\mu
, we have

\displaystyle\left|v_{\varepsilon,n,m}(t,\mu)-v_{0,n,m}(t,\mu)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}_{t}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\left|f_{n,m}^{i}\left(s,\overline{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon},\overline{\alpha}_{s}^{i}\right)-f_{n,m}^{i}\left(s,\overline{X}_{s}^{0},\overline{\alpha}_{s}^{i}\right)\right|ds+\left|g_{n,m}^{i}\left(\overline{X}_{T}^{\varepsilon}\right)-g_{n,m}^{i}\left(\overline{X}_{T}^{\varepsilon}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K}{n}\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}_{t}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\int_{t}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\overline{X}_{s}^{i;\varepsilon}-\overline{X}_{s}^{i;0}\right|\right]ds+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\overline{X}_{T}^{i;\varepsilon}-\overline{X}_{T}^{i;0}\right|\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2}K}{n^{1/2}}\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{n}_{t}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{t}^{T}T\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\overline{X}_{s}^{i;\varepsilon}-\overline{X}_{s}^{i;0}\right|^{2}\right]ds+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\overline{X}_{T}^{i;\varepsilon}-\overline{X}_{T}^{i;0}\right|^{2}\right]\right\}^{1/2}
